# Hazelnuts

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="hazelnuts-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="hazelnuts-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="hazelnuts-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Hazelnuts in the HMI library has 53 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, Se and Mo from 10 sources.<sup <ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and nickel and others, in 53 results from 10 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Akçay Kulluk D, Gökmen F, Dursun N, Özcan MM, Lemiashonak V · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Characterization of Heavy Metals in Some Edible Dried Fruits and Nuts Using Inductively Coupled Plasma Optical Emission Spectroscopy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Erwerbs-Obstbau (Springer); accepted 26 November 2022; volume and pages not printed in the filed PDF</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Puscion-Jakubik A, Markiewicz-Zukowska R, Soroczynska J, Nowakowski P, Grabia M, Mielcarek K, Przebierowska K, Kotowska K, Socha K · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Assessment of the Safe Consumption of Nuts in Terms of the Content of Toxic Elements with Chemometric Analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Pehlivan E, Arslan G, Gode F, Altun T, Özcan MM · 2008</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes">Determination of some inorganic metals in edible vegetable oils by inductively coupled plasma atomic emission spectroscopy (ICP-AES)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Grasas y Aceites</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the hazelnuts was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Akçay Kulluk D, Gökmen F, Dursun N, Özcan MM, Lemiashonak V · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Characterization of Heavy Metals in Some Edible Dried Fruits and Nuts Using Inductively Coupled Plasma Optical Emission Spectroscopy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Erwerbs-Obstbau (Springer); accepted 26 November 2022; volume and pages not printed in the filed PDF</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Puscion-Jakubik A, Markiewicz-Zukowska R, Soroczynska J, Nowakowski P, Grabia M, Mielcarek K, Przebierowska K, Kotowska K, Socha K · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Assessment of the Safe Consumption of Nuts in Terms of the Content of Toxic Elements with Chemometric Analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Pehlivan E, Arslan G, Gode F, Altun T, Özcan MM · 2008</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes">Determination of some inorganic metals in edible vegetable oils by inductively coupled plasma atomic emission spectroscopy (ICP-AES)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Grasas y Aceites</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Akçay Kulluk D, Gökmen F, Dursun N, Özcan MM, Lemiashonak V · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Characterization of Heavy Metals in Some Edible Dried Fruits and Nuts Using Inductively Coupled Plasma Optical Emission Spectroscopy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Erwerbs-Obstbau (Springer); accepted 26 November 2022; volume and pages not printed in the filed PDF</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Puscion-Jakubik A, Markiewicz-Zukowska R, Soroczynska J, Nowakowski P, Grabia M, Mielcarek K, Przebierowska K, Kotowska K, Socha K · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Assessment of the Safe Consumption of Nuts in Terms of the Content of Toxic Elements with Chemometric Analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Pehlivan E, Arslan G, Gode F, Altun T, Özcan MM · 2008</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes">Determination of some inorganic metals in edible vegetable oils by inductively coupled plasma atomic emission spectroscopy (ICP-AES)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Grasas y Aceites</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians with the basis not stated ranged from 74 µg/kg (Bielecka et al. 2021) to 110 µg/kg (Akçay et al. 2023).<sup class="cite">[[sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Akçay Kulluk D, Gökmen F, Dursun N, Özcan MM, Lemiashonak V · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Characterization of Heavy Metals in Some Edible Dried Fruits and Nuts Using Inductively Coupled Plasma Optical Emission Spectroscopy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Erwerbs-Obstbau (Springer); accepted 26 November 2022; volume and pages not printed in the filed PDF</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Puscion-Jakubik A, Markiewicz-Zukowska R, Soroczynska J, Nowakowski P, Grabia M, Mielcarek K, Przebierowska K, Kotowska K, Socha K · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Assessment of the Safe Consumption of Nuts in Terms of the Content of Toxic Elements with Chemometric Analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, 3 studies with the basis not stated reported means and medians from 3.81 µg/kg (Akçay et al. 2023) to 11.8 µg/kg (Bielecka et al. 2021).<sup class="cite">[[sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Akçay Kulluk D, Gökmen F, Dursun N, Özcan MM, Lemiashonak V · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Characterization of Heavy Metals in Some Edible Dried Fruits and Nuts Using Inductively Coupled Plasma Optical Emission Spectroscopy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Erwerbs-Obstbau (Springer); accepted 26 November 2022; volume and pages not printed in the filed PDF</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Puscion-Jakubik A, Markiewicz-Zukowska R, Soroczynska J, Nowakowski P, Grabia M, Mielcarek K, Przebierowska K, Kotowska K, Socha K · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Assessment of the Safe Consumption of Nuts in Terms of the Content of Toxic Elements with Chemometric Analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the hazelnuts came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-hazelnuts" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-hazelnuts-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of hazelnuts, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Hazelnuts, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤10</td><td>10–16 (bounded)</td><td>15–30 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤10</td><td>10–18.5 (bounded)</td><td>15.5–40 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>38 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>38</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for hazelnuts, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for hazelnuts, as sold on this basis in the completed hazelnuts ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. <a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>

No study in the library compared hazelnuts from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The HMI reference for lead in hazelnuts, as sold pools 38 subgroup units from 1 sampling family, with a median of between 10 and 16 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 10 (lower quartile) and between 15 and 30 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium in hazelnuts, as sold, the reference median was between 8.7 and 11.5 µg/kg, with a middle half from between 1 and 9.6 to between 11.5 and 13 µg/kg across 26 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For total arsenic in hazelnuts, as sold, the reference median was below 38.8 µg/kg, with a middle half from below 16 to between 2.17 and 86.5 µg/kg across 24 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, hazelnuts, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (1.9 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (10 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for hazelnuts

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing hazelnuts for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 1 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 300 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 1.2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 42 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for total arsenic ranged from 39 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 200 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

Every source record that names hazelnuts was decided on its content: 10 occurrence extracted, 10 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Hazelnuts FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-hazelnuts" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-hazelnuts" data-question="What heavy metals are found in hazelnuts?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in hazelnuts?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, Se and Mo in hazelnuts, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Akçay Kulluk D, Gökmen F, Dursun N, Özcan MM, Lemiashonak V · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Characterization of Heavy Metals in Some Edible Dried Fruits and Nuts Using Inductively Coupled Plasma Optical Emission Spectroscopy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Erwerbs-Obstbau (Springer); accepted 26 November 2022; volume and pages not printed in the filed PDF</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kulluk2023-konya-dried-fruits-nuts-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Bielecka J, Puscion-Jakubik A, Markiewicz-Zukowska R, Soroczynska J, Nowakowski P, Grabia M, Mielcarek K, Przebierowska K, Kotowska K, Socha K · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Assessment of the Safe Consumption of Nuts in Terms of the Content of Toxic Elements with Chemometric Analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Nutrients</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bielecka2021-nuts-toxic-elements-poland">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/pehlivan2008-vegetable-oils-turkey-icp-aes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Pehlivan E, Arslan G, Gode F, Altun T, Özcan regions, with subgroup medians from 1.9 to 10 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-hazelnuts" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-hazelnuts" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in hazelnuts?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in hazelnuts?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, hazelnuts, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (1.9 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (10 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-hazelnuts-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-hazelnuts-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in hazelnuts is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in hazelnuts is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 2 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 19.3 µg/kg (mean) in hazelnuts, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
